Judgement45C-7940D-0246D-0340D-0438D-0544D-0650
Group judgement

Risks

F459th of 9

Layer 3 · Risks

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

The forecasts are predictable in the order in which the lines appear in the income statement, and unpredictable in the order in which they matter to the share price. Revenue for the next 18 months is as predictable as a growth company's revenue gets: a broad European base, a new US product whose adoption is counted and running ahead of plan inside a payment window that lasts until the end of 2028, a management that guides low and delivers, and an 80% range of about 4% either side. But revenue growth does not, by itself, move EPS in this window; close to 90% of the forecast rise in FY2027 EPS depends on two things the business has not yet demonstrated, a higher margin and a lower tax rate, and a little more than half of the EPS cone's width comes from tax and currency, which are not guided, not hedged and not visible between results. The earlier sets agree with each other on this architecture, which gives it weight; where they were wrong on facts (debt, tax, the length of pass-through), the corrections almost all lower EPS and tighten the balance sheet, and I have changed five inputs accordingly, taking the model's EPS about 5% to 11% lower and to 20% (FY2026) and 35% (FY2027) below the two brokers.

Three further features shape the risk. The drivers of everything that is new in the forecast are concentrated in one product in one country, at a price we cannot verify and under a leadership arrangement we cannot confirm. The company reports twice a year, so the cone narrows in three steps rather than steadily, and by most on 2 March 2027, when the tax charge, the margin wording and the year-end TOUCH run-rate arrive together. And the share price is 94% company-specific, so earnings risk and multiple risk are one risk, with no sector floor under a multiple twice the sector's. My steer to the Consumer: present the case as two forecasts of different quality, a revenue forecast that deserves high confidence and an earnings forecast that deserves low confidence until the first of the two undemonstrated strengths shows; and treat 2 March 2027 as the date on which the predictability of the case is itself re-rated, in either direction.

C-79

How narrow is the business perimeter (number of countries and business lines), how is it changing, and what does that mean for predictability?

F4032nd of 36

Layer 1 · Company - Economic strength

Judgement. The perimeter is moderately broad and widening, which lowers predictability. Medartis now runs three brands (plus CADskills), three reporting segments, four regions, about 12 direct subsidiaries and some 50 distributor markets, and five or six production sites in four countries, with new LATAM countries being added. Each extra moving part adds a source of forecast error (Japan, Brazil, Mexico, Australia have each caused misses or surprises in the last four years), while the growth itself is concentrated in two places (the US and TOUCH).

For the model. Forecast by region and product, not only at group level: the US and KeriMedical/TOUCH as separate high-growth lines; EMEA plating as the large, steady base; APAC and LATAM as volatile, policy-driven lines. Accept a wider error band than for a single-product, single-site company.

D-02

To what extent does past and present fundamental delivery make the expected change plausible, and to what extent does the thesis rely only on strengths the business demonstrably has, rather than on strengths it would need to acquire?

F469th of 9

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement. The revenue forecast rests on strengths Medartis has demonstrated; the earnings forecast rests mostly on strengths it has not. I can put numbers on the split. Holding the FY2026 margin and tax rate constant, revenue growth alone would take EPS from about CHF 1.24 in FY2026 to about CHF 1.85 in FY2029; the forecast is CHF 3.20. So about 30% of the forecast EPS growth to FY2029 comes from the demonstrated strength (growing revenue in the mid-teens), about 35% from the EBITDA margin rising by 2.8 points (partly mechanical, mostly a choice management has never yet made), and about 35% from the tax rate falling from 42% to 26% (not demonstrated at all). In the 18-month window the reliance is more extreme: revenue growth alone adds only about CHF 0.05 to FY2027 EPS, because the extra gross profit is absorbed by CHF 4.5m more depreciation and by the loss of FY2026's currency gain. Close to 90% of the forecast growth in FY2027 EPS (CHF 1.24 to CHF 1.63) depends on margin release and a lower tax rate. That is the single most important thing to understand about the predictability of the next 18 months: the predictable line does not, by itself, move EPS.

The useful way to see the business: a Bonesupport-like product inside a Medacta-like company.

  • The product. TOUCH in the US is a reimbursed, clinically differentiated, single device being adopted surgeon by surgeon through a payment window, with a high gross margin and no direct rival. The closest precedent is Bonesupport's CERAMENT G: US adoption ran ahead of plan, paused for one quarter in 2026 when hospital administrators imposed pre-approvals, and resumed at +49% once the payment rules improved, with an adjusted EBIT margin of 34%. The base rate for this kind of product compounding through its payment window is good, and Medartis's own evidence fits it: US procedure projections for 2026 raised from 1,200 to 1,800 to about 3,000; more than 180 US surgeons trained in H1 2026 against a 330 target; ten years of European use; 75% of French thumb-base operations.
  • The company. Everything around the product is the Medacta model: a Swiss-franc cost base, instrument sets placed at the company's expense, surgeon education as the selling engine, growth through independent agents in the US. The base rate for that body releasing margin and cash while growing fast is poor. Medacta's adjusted EBITDA margin fell from 29.5% (2019) to 26.3% (2023) while it grew at a double-digit rate; its free cash flow was about zero in 2021, negative in 2023 and EUR 15.7m in 2025; when its growth slowed in 2026 it dropped its margin-expansion target within months. Straumann, whose former finance director is now Medartis's, set its own mid-term target at 0.4 to 0.5 points of margin a year, below what analysts wanted, precisely to keep room to reinvest.

The forecast borrows its revenue from the product and its margins, cash and tax from an expectation that the company will behave differently from its closest precedent. That is possible (Medartis starts from 18%, not 28%, so it has more scale benefit ahead of it), but it is a strength to be acquired.

The strengths the forecast needs, sorted.

Strength the forecast needs Demonstrated? Evidence Forecast lines that rest on it
Growing above the market through training and share gain Yes Organic 11.7%, 15.7%, 17.0%; market about 9% Revenue in all years
Guiding conservatively and delivering Yes, for two years; FY2026 was helped by mechanical tailwinds FY2025 and FY2026 raised at the half; FY2027 is the first year with hard comparisons Revenue FY2027
Winning approval, payment and early adoption for TOUCH in the US Yes Approval July 2025; pass-through 2026 to 2028; about 3,000 procedures in year one US revenue 2027-28
Acting fast on structural problems Yes Distributor reset, Project Flash, NeoOrtho integration in ten months Gross margin (tariff, Warsaw)
Holding gross margin through shocks Yes 81% to 83% through tariffs and franc strength; 81.5% in H1 2026 Gross margin 82.3% to 83%
Releasing margin while growing fast No Core EBITDA margin never held above 19%; selling and distribution rose to 37.1% of sales in H1 2026 from 36.4%; guidance wording caps it EBITDA margin from FY2027; one-third of EPS growth
Turning growth into cash No Free cash flow CHF 21.0m, 9.3m, then -10.1m in H1 2026; bank debt up from CHF 32m to 58m in six months Net debt; funding of earn-outs and NeoOrtho
Lowering the tax rate No 46% in FY2025 and again in H1 2026 at twice the profit One-third of EPS growth
Selling plates to TOUCH accounts Not yet Management: "still building"; no account data US growth excluding TOUCH from 2027
Holding the US TOUCH price after pass-through Untested until 2029 Precedents: volumes hold, price concessions of 20% to 25% are common Revenue and margin 2029-30
Stable US leadership during a launch Unclear See D-01 US execution

Two points that sharpen the picture.

  1. The US growth record is narrower than it looks. H1 2026 US organic growth of 27.4% was an increase of about CHF 6.5m at constant currency. About 900 TOUCH procedures (Set 2's inference) at USD 4,250 to 6,500 are worth CHF 3m to 4.7m of that. If, as I believe, those sales are counted as organic, US growth excluding TOUCH was only about 7% to 14%, against 13.4% in FY2025 (about 18% excluding the Florida disruption). The rebuilt plating channel has not yet shown the growth the US ambition (above 20%) assumes once TOUCH annualises; the demonstrated US strength is TOUCH.
  2. Why tax has not normalised, and what would make it. The largest item in the tax reconciliation is tax paid on profit booked when the Swiss and French plants sell to the group's own subsidiaries, with no deferred tax asset recognised. That item grows whenever inventory and instrument sets held abroad grow, which is whenever the group grows. It is the same loop that consumes the cash: the inventory build that absorbs working capital also inflates the tax rate. It eases when inventory abroad stops rising faster than sales, when US plating is made in Indiana (from 2027), and when loss-making subsidiaries earn enough to use CHF 22.5m of unrecognised losses. Each is plausible; none has started to show. Management's 18% to 19% is a destination, not a record.

Peer base rate for the year after the peak. Of the specialists in the reports, growth slowed sharply after the acceleration year at Medacta (18.5% to 9.7%), Treace (to a decline) and Globus (guidance cut); it held at Paragon 28 (18%) and Bonesupport (25% to 30%). The two that held had a protected, differentiated product still early in its adoption. TOUCH puts Medartis in that group for US revenue; EMEA plating (56% of sales) is closer to the first group.

D-03In relation to the differences between the forecasts over the next 18 months to four years, vs. the last four to six years' financials, how diffuse or concentrated are the key drivers that generate the differences?F408th of 9full answer under 2a Internal risks
D-04

How wide is the cone of plausible outcomes over the next 18 months, how skewed is it, and so how predictable is the case?

F388th of 9

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement. The cone is narrow for revenue, moderate for the EBITDA margin and wide for EPS, and the three have different skews. Over the 18 months to the FY2027 results (March 2028), my 80% ranges for FY2027 are: revenue within about 4% of CHF 394m; core EBITDA margin between 18.5% and 20.1%; EPS between about CHF 1.0 and CHF 2.3 around a base of CHF 1.63, that is, roughly minus 38% to plus 44%. A little more than half of the width of the EPS cone comes from two lines below EBIT, currency on intercompany loans and the tax rate, which the company neither guides nor hedges and which cannot be observed between results. The case is therefore predictable on operations and unpredictable on earnings. The two brokers' FY2027 EPS of CHF 2.50 lies above the top of my range.

The cone, at each of the three results dates in the window.

A striking feature of the nearest event. With six weeks of FY2026 left, its revenue is known to within about 1.5%, but its EPS only to within about 20%, because the H2 tax charge could be anything from about CHF 2m (the 13% rate the brokers' number implies) to about CHF 6.5m (H1's 46% repeated), and because the franc's move in H2 on more than CHF 200m of intercompany loans is not yet reported.

What makes the FY2027 EPS cone wide (each item's effect on EPS of CHF 1.63).

Source Plausible swing Effect on EPS Observable before the results?
Currency on intercompany loans plus or minus CHF 6m plus or minus 0.31 Partly (exchange rates are public; the loan mix is not)
Tax rate 26% to 42% against 35% +0.23 to -0.18 No

Depreciation plus or minus CHF 1.5m plus or minus 0.08 No

The skews, and why they differ.

  • Revenue: even around my base. Set 2 called the 18-month revenue skew upward. For FY2026 I agree. For FY2027 I see it as even, for four reasons: it is the first year without mechanical help (KeriMedical entering the organic base, the easy Florida comparison and the winter-trauma half all fall away); the sector's US elective market has weakened since Set 1 (Medacta's warning of 5 November names insurer pre-authorisation and deductibles, and a thumb-base replacement is an elective operation); the 7,000 US TOUCH procedures need the monthly rate to keep rising through 2027 from a year-end rate I estimate at 250 to 500 a month (the company's "about 3,000" for 2026 implies up to roughly 1,000 procedures in the last two months of the year, after "more than 2,000" by October); and the price per procedure is unverified. Against company guidance the skew is up; against the two brokers it is down.
  • Margin: down. Beats are reinvested (about 20% reaches EBITDA); shortfalls drop through at 50% to 60% because the cost base for high-teens growth is committed (Set 2, P-01). I have nothing to add except that Medacta's November warning is a live example of this asymmetry in the closest peer.
  • EPS: even around my base, with fat tails. On the upside, management may be right about tax (each five points is CHF 0.13) and may let margin through. On the downside, a revenue shortfall, a franc spike and an unchanged tax rate are each worth CHF 0.2 to 0.4, and the first two tend to arrive together with a weak US dollar.
  • Balance sheet: towards more debt. Earn-outs, the investment peak and negative cash flow all point one way; at about CHF 165m of net financial debt at end-2027 the known bank facilities would be nearly used.

How the cone evolves. It does not narrow steadily. It narrows in three steps, on the three results dates, and by most on the first: 2 March 2027 will fix the FY2026 tax charge, the margin wording for FY2027, the year-end TOUCH run-rate and the debt position. I estimate that one event removes about a third of the FY2027 EPS uncertainty. Between events there are no company data (D-05).

Sudden events that would widen it (each low probability; Set 1's C-81 lists them): a safety or revision signal on TOUCH in the US, a halt at Basel, a franc spike, a US administrator pause of the Bonesupport kind (one quarter's pause is worth three to four points of group growth in a half), or a failure in the US channel.

D-05

How helpful is the company's disclosure on operating momentum and key drivers of the thesis to monitor the case over about the next six months?

F448th of 9

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement. Helpful in content, unhelpful in frequency, and silent on what decides EPS. In the next six months the company is scheduled to report once, on 2 March 2027 (FY2026 results, the annual report and the first FY2027 guidance). There are no quarterly sales figures. On that one day the disclosure on operating momentum will be good by the standard of small European medical-technology companies; between now and then, and for five and a half months after it, the thesis can be followed only through indirect evidence and whatever ad hoc statements the company chooses to make (it has made one each November: an investor presentation on 19 November 2025 and the TOUCH release of 2 November 2026). The revenue leg of the thesis can be monitored; the earnings leg can only be audited after the fact.

What the company does disclose, and how well it maps to the thesis drivers.

  • Disclosed and useful: organic growth by region and by product group; cumulative US TOUCH procedures and the year's projection; US surgeons trained; the share of US volume made in Indiana; tariff cost in francs and its lag through inventory; the currency drag on the margin in basis points; core gross margin and EBITDA margin with a bridge from IFRS; capital spending, working capital and free cash flow.
  • Not disclosed, and decisive: TOUCH revenue, price or margin; US growth excluding TOUCH and any measure of selling plates to TOUCH accounts; the split of selling cost between commission and fixed cost; the tax rate expected for the year; the currency mix of the intercompany loans; bank covenants; the triggers and timing of CHF 20m of earn-outs; distributor or representative turnover in the US.

What to read on 2 March 2027, in order of information value for the forecasts.

  1. The FY2026 core tax charge, and anything said about the FY2027 rate. This settles the largest open question in FY2026 and FY2027 EPS.
  2. The margin wording for FY2027: "high teens at constant currency" again, or something higher (I-01).
  3. The FY2027 organic growth range.
  4. US TOUCH: procedures in 2026, the fourth-quarter rate, any 2027 projection, any statement on price.
  5. US growth excluding TOUCH, or any evidence of plate sales into TOUCH accounts.
  6. Selling and distribution cost as a share of sales in H2 2026 (37.1% in H1; Set 2's margin path needs it to start falling).
  7. Bank debt, facilities and earn-out payments in the annual report; whether the tariff refund of CHF 4.4m was collected.
  8. Who is shown as leading the US business.

What can be watched between results.

  • Exchange rates, daily: the dollar, euro, Australian dollar, Brazilian real and Mexican peso against the franc give the translation effect on sales and a rough estimate of the gain or loss on intercompany loans (the H1 2026 gain came from the real, the peso and the Australian dollar).
  • Peers' quarterly reports (late January to February, and late April to May 2027): Stryker's trauma and extremities and Zimmer Biomet's Paragon 28 for the extremities market; Bonesupport for US hospital administrators' behaviour towards a reimbursed specialist device; Medacta for US elective demand; Straumann for the franc.
  • US Medicare rule-making: the outpatient payment rule for 2028 (proposed about July 2027, final about November 2027) for any change to TOUCH's code or payment group. The rule that sets payment after pass-through is the 2029 rule, proposed in mid-2028.
  • US regulatory databases for a rival thumb-base prosthesis and for adverse-event reports on TOUCH.
  • Company releases on appointments (a US President) and on TOUCH milestones; filings of share sales by the founder.

Against peers. Straumann and Bonesupport report quarterly; Medacta reports half-yearly but issues trading updates. Medartis is at the low end for frequency and about average for content. For a stock whose share price is 94% company-specific (D-07), two data points a year means information arrives in jumps: the shares rose by about a fifth after the 2 November statement (from about CHF 91, their 50-day average, to CHF 109).

D-06

How clearly does what the company says it will achieve trace into specific operational drivers, onto identifiable revenue, cost and margin lines (the key fundamental milestones and measures), and through to earnings per share; and how clear is the external transmission from value-chain dynamics to earnings per share?

D505th of 9

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement. The trace is clear and quantified from the company's statements to revenue and gross margin, becomes a stated outcome without a bridge at EBITDA, and stops before EPS. Medartis tells investors what it will sell and roughly what its gross margin will bear; it tells them the EBITDA margin as a single phrase; and it tells them nothing about depreciation, the financial result, tax or EPS. So the milestones that can be named and dated (TOUCH procedures, surgeons trained, local production, tariff cost) all sit above the line where the forecast's difference from the past is generated. External transmission is clear in direction for all three forces that matter (reimbursement, tariffs, the franc) and measurable for the first two.

The internal chain, link by link.

A ready reckoner for the Consumer (FY2027, my arithmetic on the changed inputs; CHF 1m of pre-tax profit is about CHF 0.05 of EPS).

Driver Change EPS effect
Organic growth 1 point (CHF 3.4m of revenue) +0.04 if above plan (reinvested); -0.10 if below
US TOUCH 1,000 procedures (about CHF 5.3m at USD 6,500; about 1.5 points of group growth) about 0.11, or 7%, on my assumed 40% contribution; about two-thirds of that at USD 4,250

The table shows the problem in one view: the drivers the company discloses most carefully (growth, procedures) have the smallest EPS effect per unit of surprise, and the ones it does not guide (tax, currency) have the largest.

The external chain.

  • Reimbursement to EPS: clear and dated. Medicare pass-through (to 31 December 2028) removes the hospital's cost objection; hospital committees then take up to four months each; trained surgeons begin operating; procedures become revenue at the US price. Each step is named by the company and the lag is known. The weak link is the last one, price.
  • Tariffs to EPS: clear and quantified. Tariff rate on imports, held in inventory for 6 to 10 months, then cost of goods; removed as production moves to Indiana.
  • The franc to EPS: clear in direction, through three channels of different visibility. Translation of sales (4.4 points of growth in FY2025); the margin effect of franc costs against foreign revenue (about 1.2 points in FY2025; 90 to 100 basis points guided for FY2026); and revaluation of intercompany loans in the financial result (a loss of CHF 5.2m in FY2025, a net gain of about CHF 3m in H1 2026). The first two are disclosed; the third is disclosed only after the event.
  • Competition and buyers to EPS: not traceable. Contract price-downs of 1% to 3% a year are known; the effect of Acumed's and Stryker's launches on share cannot be read from any disclosed figure until it shows in regional growth.

Two practical consequences. First, a "beat" or "miss" on EPS in FactSet may be an artefact of basis: for FY2025 FactSet recorded CHF 0.184 as the actual on the brokers' basis against core EPS of about CHF 0.49. Second, the reported organic growth rate in H2 2026 includes KeriMedical for the first time, so part of the acceleration the company will report on 2 March is definitional.

C-82

What perception- or sentiment-driven risks are there that are sudden and high-magnitude, including events elsewhere in the sector, theme or market that could hit the share price whatever the company's own fundamentals?

D3426th of 36

Layer 1 · Risks

Judgement. Sentiment risk is high. The stock has re-rated sharply (+45% in twelve months, +16.6% in three months, a gap up after the 2 November statement), is the most highly rated stock on P/E in its sector (36.9x current against a sector median of 17.1x), is small and illiquid (USD 2.4m to 4.2m traded a day), has thin coverage, and sits in a market regime the Fitness for Fighting digest describes as risk-off beneath an index at its high, with Fragility at Double Underweight. Read-across events that could hit it: a deceleration or warning at Medacta, Stryker, Zimmer Biomet or Acumed's peers; US hospital capital or procedure softness (already visible in large joints); a franc spike; a sharp rise in yields; a founder block sale; the buy-share fall from 100% to 75% over three months turning into a downgrade cluster. The pinned technical momentum letter is D (dashboard, 15 November 2026) despite price strength (88th percentile relative strength, price 28% above the 200-day average), which flags that the move is extended.

Layer 1 · Guidance

Judgement. Under the present CEO the behaviour is a consistent "raise at the half, beat at the year-end" pattern on the top line, plus operating-target upgrades during the year and an unscheduled positive trading statement. Margin guidance is never upgraded. Before him, guidance was cut (FY2024 from mid-teens to 12% to 15%, then missed at 11.7%). The CEO's stated philosophy ("we deliver what we promise") and the Straumann institutional habit (Straumann has beaten or met guidance in almost every recent period; a "classic sandbagger") suggest this is deliberate and likely to persist.

Evidence. FY2025: 13% to 15% raised to 14% to 16%, delivered 15.7%. FY2026: 16% to 18% raised to 17% to 19%, then "well positioned to exceed" (2 November 2026). TOUCH US units: 1,000 (first-year projection) to 1,200 to 1,800 to more than 2,000 surgeries by October. Core EBITDA margin: "high teens" held every time; delivered 18.4% (FY2025) and 18.0% (H1 2026).

C-69

What is the company's record of missing, meeting or beating guidance and sell-side estimates over the last one, three and five years, including in comparable environmental conditions?

D5422nd of 36

Layer 1 · Sell-side estimates

Judgement. Revenue: recently reliable and improving (beat or met in each period since H1 2025). Profit and EPS: unreliable, with large misses driven by financing, currency and non-core items, including in the most comparable recent environment (franc strength plus tariffs in 2025).

  • Last year. FY2025 (reported March 2026): core sales CHF 266.1m against consensus of about CHF 263.5m and growth of 15.7% against 15.0% expected (beat); core EBITDA margin 18.4% against 18.3% (in line); core net profit CHF 6.1m against CHF 9.5m consensus (a 36% miss, from CHF 6.8m convertible cost and CHF 5.3m of FX losses), followed by double-digit EPS downgrades. H1 2026: organic 17.0% against a mid-teens consensus (beat); core EBITDA margin 18.0% (in line with high-teens guidance); core net profit CHF 6.8m (flattered by FX gains). November 2026: guidance to be exceeded.
  • Three years. FY2023 broadly met (organic about 17% within 15% to 18% per the memo; core EBITDA ahead of target); FY2024 missed a lowered range (11.7% against 12% to 15%, after cutting from mid-teens), though core EBITDA margin rose to 19%; FY2025 beat on sales, missed on profit.
  • Five years. 2019 miss and margin fall; 2020 Covid (sales -1.9%); 2021 rebound (+25%); 2022 China exit and IFRS net loss; 2023 ransomware (-3% H1 sales).
  • EPS estimate history. FactSet's FY2025 consensus EPS fell 77.8% over the twelve months before results (range CHF 0.15 to 1.27); FY2026 is down 11.5% over twelve months but up 8.9% in the last month.
P-06

After assessing the track record of delivery in greater detail, how much weight should the forecasts for the next 18 months to four years carry, and in which direction should they be leaned?

C559th of 23

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement. Weight the forecasts by line, not as a whole. Over 18 months, revenue deserves high weight (an 80% range of about ±4% around my base) and should be leaned slightly upward against my base and against company guidance, but not against consensus, which is already above both. The EBITDA margin deserves medium weight and no lean: this management delivers "high teens" precisely and has never chosen to exceed it. EPS deserves low weight (an 80% range of about ±25% to 30% at 18 months) and should be leaned downward against consensus, because below-EBIT items (tax, currency, financing) have produced the company's largest misses. Over two to four years, revenue carries medium weight, margin low-to-medium, EPS low, and free cash flow low.

The record by line.

Period Organic growth: guidance and delivery Margin: guidance and delivery Profit against consensus
FY2019 (old management) Shortfall (8% group growth) EBITDA margin about 15% (H1 13%) Profit warning
FY2023 15% to 18% guided; 17.4% delivered 13% to 15% guided; 15.9% delivered Broadly met
FY2024 Cut from mid-teens to 12% to 15%; 11.7% delivered (missed) 19.0% delivered

FY2026 16% to 18%, raised to 17% to 19%; "well positioned to exceed" (2 November 2026) "High teens at constant currency"; 18.0% in H1 FY2026 consensus EPS down 11.5% over twelve months, up 8.9% over one

Why revenue carries high weight and leans up. Under the present CEO (two years) the top line has been raised at the half and beaten at the year-end twice, and operating targets (TOUCH units) were raised three times; the Straumann-trained team's institutional habit is to guide conservatively. My FY2026 base (organic about 19.5% to 20%, reported +25.7%) already sits above the top of guidance; the lean up is about 1 to 2 percentage points of growth, worth about CHF 3m to 7m of revenue in FY2027. The caution: both beat years enjoyed mechanical tailwinds (KeriMedical entering the organic base, the easy Florida comparison, winter trauma in Europe); FY2027, with harder comparisons, is the first year that tests whether the record is management or arithmetic.

Why the margin carries medium weight and no lean. Delivered core EBITDA margins of 18.4% (FY2025) and 18.0% (H1 2026) against "high teens" guidance: no misses, no beats. The habit makes the near-term margin unusually predictable on the downside of the brokers (it rarely surprises up) and within about ±1 point of my base, unless revenue falls short (P-01's asymmetry).

Why EPS carries low weight and leans down against consensus. The FY2025 profit miss came entirely below EBIT (CHF 6.8m of convertible cost and CHF 5.3m of currency losses, then a core tax charge of CHF 5.2m); H1 2026's improvement was helped by currency gains (the financial result was CHF 6.7m better). The two brokers assume a tax rate of about 21% in FY2027 against an observed core rate of about 46%, and depreciation below the H1 2026 run-rate. Leaning down does not mean expecting a miss of the company's own targets (it gives no EPS guidance); it means the consensus EPS line is more likely to be revised down than up over 18 months, even while revenue is revised up.

Weight beyond 18 months. Two years of delivery by a new team is a short record; the earlier record (2019 miss, 2022 China exit and loss, 2023 ransomware, 2024 cut and miss, the NSI write-off) belongs to different management but the same business model, and shows that the model produces an incident every year or two. For 2028-2030 I would weight revenue at about ±10% to 12%, EBITDA margin ±2 points, EPS ±40%.

Group judgement

Financial forecasts

D5212th of 23

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

The operating and financial model for Medartis is a story of a strong, fairly predictable top line feeding a margin and earnings break-out whose timing is more uncertain than its direction. Revenue should compound at about 16% a year for four years on drivers that are visible half by half (US TOUCH procedures, trained surgeons, local production, regional organic growth), and the reinforcing US training-to-adoption loop gives 2027-2028 more visibility than a single-product ramp would normally have. Below gross profit the business should finally leave the 15% to 19% EBITDA and 9% EBIT range it has occupied for six years: the leverage is real, its rate (about 25% to 29% incremental EBITDA margin) is defensible against the long-run 21.5% plus identifiable tailwinds, and it shows more in EBIT than in EBITDA because the depreciation ratio falls after the 2025-27 investment peak. Three things temper the picture. First, the leverage is asymmetric: beats are reinvested under a "high teens" guidance habit, shortfalls drop through at about 50%. Second, more than a quarter of EPS growth comes from below-EBIT normalisation, of which the largest piece, a core tax rate falling from about 46% towards the low 20s, is unproven; currency on CHF 200m of unhedged intercompany loans can move a year's EPS by 20% to 40%. Third, the business consumes its cash to grow until 2028, carrying net financial debt of CHF 117m to 128m and leaving no room for a sizeable acquisition without new capital. Against the market's numbers, my base case agrees on FY2026 and on cash, but places FY2027-28 EPS 30% to 35% below the two brokers because consensus needs revenue, margin, depreciation and tax all at their favourable ends. For an investment case, the forecasts are positive for the durability of growth and for the eventual earnings level, and neutral-to-negative for the next 18 months of earnings revisions.

C-49What earnings-per-share growth is the company generating, with what revenue growth, and how durable is it?D5022nd of 36full answer under 4c Financial profile
P-08

After assessing below-operating-profit cost items (e.g. tax, interest, one-offs, minority investments, etc.) and share buybacks or issuance in greater detail, how is it likely to shape earnings per share, net income and free cash flow over the next 18 months to four years, versus the last four to six years?

F4218th of 23

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement. Below-EBIT items will move from a crushing drag to a moderate one: in FY2025 only a quarter of core EBIT reached core net profit (CHF 24.5m to CHF 6.1m, 75% leakage); in my FY2030 two-thirds does (CHF 81.4m to CHF 54.5m, 33% leakage). That shift is why EPS grows about 35% a year from 2026 to 2030 against about 25% EBIT growth. But it is the least predictable part of the model and should be leaned down against consensus in 2026-2028, because the three largest items are the tax rate (unproven normalisation), currency on intercompany loans (unforecastable, unhedged) and fixed financing cost (certain, and larger than the brokers assume relative to EBIT). There will be no buybacks or dividends; share issuance is limited to employee plans (about 0.3% a year) unless the convertible is called from May 2029 (P-02). Free cash flow lags net income badly until 2029 because growth absorbs working capital and capex.

Item by item.

Tax, the largest and least proven. FY2025 core tax CHF 5.2m on core pre-tax profit of about CHF 11.3m (about 46%); IFRS 27% in 2025 after -41% in 2024; FactSet's reported rates swung from -55% to +70% over seven years. The likely causes: losses in entities without recognised deferred tax (US, Japan, LATAM expansion), transfer-pricing adjustments (2025), non-deductible items. Management expects an 18% to 19% underlying rate and calls sell-side rates above 19% too conservative; there are no advance pricing agreements; the profit mix is shifting towards higher-tax jurisdictions (US, France for KeriMedical, Brazil) from Basel; Pillar Two sets a 15% floor. The brokers imply 29% (FY2026), 21% (FY2027), 20% (FY2028). My path is 38%, 28%, 25%, 23%, 22%: normalisation as profits outgrow the fixed leakage, but slower than management says. Each 5 points of tax rate in FY2027 is about CHF 1.6m of net profit, about CHF 0.13 of EPS (7%). If management is right (about 19% from FY2027), my FY2027 EPS rises from CHF 1.76 to about CHF 1.99.

Currency on intercompany loans, the most volatile. More than CHF 200m of intercompany loans in foreign currency are revalued every period: FY2025 a CHF 5.2m loss, H1 2026 a CHF 7.0m gain on intercompany balances (Brazilian real and Australian dollar). The disclosed sensitivities (10% USD/CHF about CHF 1.9m of pre-tax profit; AUD/CHF CHF 1.3m; EUR/CHF CHF 0.4m) understate it. I assume about CHF +3m net for FY2026 (H1's gain, nothing further in H2) and CHF -1m a year after (a small negative bias, because the franc tends to appreciate and banks forecast a weak Australian dollar; Morgan Stanley sees USD/CHF at 0.82 through 2027). The realistic band is ±CHF 6m a year, equal to 20% to 40% of 2026-27 net profit.

Fixed financing. About CHF 10m to 11m a year (P-02), of which CHF 3.4m to 3.7m is non-cash accretion; it is near-fixed in nominal terms, so its share of EBIT halves by 2030.

Minorities. 49% of NeoOrtho's profit (NeoOrtho contributed about CHF 1.0m of net profit in eight months of 2025; about CHF 1.4m annualised) goes to minorities until the buy-out: about CHF 0.8m to 1.1m a year in 2026-2028, half a year in 2029, nil in 2030. The buy-out (CHF 18.4m liability at June 2026; up to CHF 24.8m undiscounted, based on FY2028 sales) is a FY2029 cash outflow.

Non-core items. Core excludes them, but they cost cash and appear in IFRS: CHF 1.9m of CADskills transaction and legal costs in H1 2026, step-up and acquisition amortisation (about CHF 2m a year). I assume CHF 2.5m of non-core cash cost in 2026 and about CHF 0.5m to 1m a year after. IFRS EPS will sit about CHF 0.1 to 0.2 below core EPS each year.

Capitalised development. CHF 4.3m capitalised in 2025 against CHF 0.8m amortised; I assume about CHF 4.5m to 5m a year capitalised and amortisation rising by about CHF 0.5m to 1m a year. It flatters EBITDA by about CHF 3.5m and EBIT by less each year; a write-down (as NSI's CHF 17.9m in 2024) is the tail risk.

Share count. Basic about 12.43m (2026) rising by about 0.04m a year from employee plans (36,737 shares issued in 2025; 13,836 in H1 2026) to about 12.6m in 2030. No buybacks (none ever; cash is constrained), no dividend (none ever). An equity raise is plausible only to fund a large acquisition.

C-78

Beyond cutting costs, how much plausible scope is there to improve the financial outputs over the mid term; do peer precedents show it is achievable; would it come from a few big areas or many small ones; and is it congruent with the stated strategy?

B666th of 36

Layer 1 · Company - Economic strength

Judgement. Plausible scope is substantial: from an 18% core EBITDA and 9% EBIT margin to something like 22% to 24% EBITDA and 13% to 15% EBIT by 2028/29, plus a working-capital and capex normalisation that would turn free cash flow clearly positive. It would come from a few big areas (selling-cost leverage on scale, gross-margin recovery, capex normalisation) and several small ones (Cold Fusion, procurement, Titan Nail, tariff refund, interest share of EBIT falling). Peer precedent shows the level is achievable for specialist orthopaedics at about EUR 600m to 700m of sales (Medacta at 27% to 29% adjusted EBITDA). It is congruent with the stated strategy ("growth has to come with profitability"; opex ratio and cash flow in pay) but conflicts with the guidance habit of holding margins at "high teens".

The big areas, sized roughly.

  • Selling and distribution leverage: each point of sales growth above cost growth over three years could add 2 to 4 points of margin as US territories mature and training infrastructure is built.
  • Gross margin: +1 to +2 points from tariff removal (about 0.8 points in 2027 alone), Warsaw scale, TOUCH mix and own products, minus NeoOrtho growth.
  • Capex from about 10% to 6% to 8% of sales after 2026/27 (the brokers model CHF 35m, CHF 34m, CHF 29m); NWC intensity from 47% towards 40%.
  • Below EBIT: interest falling from about 28% to about 10% of EBIT as EBIT grows; currency neutral at best.

Caution. Brokers already embed much of this (EBITDA margin 19.0%, 20.1%, 21.3%, 22.4% for FY2026 to FY2029 on their basis). The scope exists; the question for Set 2 is timing and whether management releases it or reinvests it.

C-80

To what extent has the core growth engine decelerated over the last twelve months: is the revenue growth rate running below the company's own normal rate, and is the input change force itself slowing?

B+725th of 36

Layer 1 · Risks

Judgement. It has not decelerated; it has accelerated, and the input forces (TOUCH adoption, the rebuilt US channel) are strengthening. Organic growth is running above the company's own normal rate (five-year reported CAGR of about 16% FY2020 to FY2025; organic 11.7% in FY2024, 15.7% in FY2025, 17.0% in H1 2026, guided 17% to 19% and likely to be exceeded). Beneath the headline, three sub-engines have slowed: APAC (5.5% organic), LATAM organic (9.7%) and lower extremities (10.4% from 15.1%), and part of EMEA's H1 2026 strength was weather. The growth engine is therefore narrowing towards the US and upper extremities, which raises concentration risk even as the headline improves.

Monitorables. The Risks report's re-pre-mortem trigger (US organic growth below 10%) is far away at 27.4%. The disconfirming threshold it set for the bear case (US growth above 20% within two quarters) has been met.

EBIT margin Core 9% to 9.5%; FactSet adjusted 0% to 7% 9.9%, 11.9%, 14.0% Plausible only with selling-cost leverage and tariff removal
EPS Core CHF 0.35, 0.92, 0.49 (FY2023 to FY2025); FY2025 consensus fell 78% in a year CHF 1.55, 2.50, 3.79 (+61%, +52%) Highly sensitive to below-EBIT items and the convertible
Capex CHF 11m to 26m, 5% to 10% of sales CHF 35m, 34m, 29m Credible; shows the investment peak in 2026/27
Free cash flow CHF 21m, 9m, -10m (H1 2026) CHF 3m, 10m, 30m Credible in direction; depends on NWC
Dividend none none Credible

Granular working. The brokers' FY2027 EBIT rise of CHF 16m on CHF 74m more sales (22% incremental EBIT margin) is the key assumption. Supporting it: about CHF 3m of tariff cost disappears; TOUCH mix lifts gross margin; capex and set investments slow. Against it: the history of reinvestment; currency (a further franc rise would take 0.5 to 1 point off reported margins); higher depreciation from the 2025/26 capex wave (consensus D&A rises from CHF 27.6m to CHF 30.8m). Segment consensus is aggressive in upper extremities (CHF 242m to 307m to 376m, +27% and +22%) and in North America (CHF 69m to 98m to 129m), which implies a very large TOUCH ramp; the TOUCH US average selling price, not given in any source, is the single unknown that most decides whether this is realistic.

P-04After assessing the pricing power in greater detail, how is price likely to contribute to revenue and margin over the next 18 months to four years?C508th of 23full answer under 5a Pricing power
P-09

After assessing the other questions/items in the set in greater detail, what is the net likely impact to the main financial line items over the next 18 months to four years, versus the last four to six years?

D5212th of 23

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement. The next four years look like the last five on revenue and unlike them on everything below gross profit. Revenue keeps compounding at about 16% a year (FY2025-FY2030 CAGR 16.3%, against 16.0% for FY2020-FY2025 and 12.9% for FY2019-FY2025), peaking at about 22% reported in H2 2026 and settling at 12% by FY2030. Below gross profit the business breaks out of its historical range for the first time: core EBITDA margin above 19% from FY2027 and about 22% by FY2030 (range FY2019-25: 15% to 19%), core EBIT margin from about 9% to about 14%, EPS up nine-fold from the FY2025 core base of CHF 0.49 to about CHF 4.3, and free cash flow after leases turning sustainably positive from FY2028. The net impact is driven by a few identifiable items, and their skews differ: revenue skewed up near term, margin skewed down relative to the brokers (reinvestment) but up relative to history, EPS skewed down against consensus but up against my base if tax normalises as management says.

Line by line.

Line Last four to six years Next four years (my base) Net change and its source
Revenue growth Reported +25%, +14%, +16%, +6%, +20% (FY2021-25); organic 11.7% to 17% +25.7% (FY2026, incl. acquisitions), then +16.5%, +14.5%, +13.0%, +12.0% Similar rate; source shifts to US TOUCH and US plating (US from 19% of sales to about 27% by 2028)
Core gross margin 81% to 83%, eroded in 2025 81.8% rising to 83.0% +1 point: TOUCH mix, tariff roll-off, Warsaw, own products, less NeoOrtho
Core EBITDA margin 15% to 19%; 18.1% FY2025 18.6%, 19.5%, 20.5%, 21.4%, 22.2% +4 points: about half scale on selling and administration, a third mix and absorption, the rest efficiency
Core EBIT margin about 9% 9.9% to 14.2% +5 points: EBITDA gains plus a falling depreciation ratio after the capex peak
Below-EBIT leakage 45% (FY2024), 75% (FY2025) 52% falling to 33% Tax normalising (largest, least proven), fixed financing diluted by growth, NeoOrtho minority bought out
Core EPS 0.35, 0.92, 0.49 (FY2023-25) 1.31, 1.76, 2.48, 3.35, 4.33 Nine-fold; 15% to 35% below the two brokers

Working capital, % of sales 47% to 57% 44.5% falling to 41% About a third of incremental sales

Where the net change concentrates. Of the roughly CHF 182m of revenue added between FY2025 and FY2028, the US contributes about CHF 68m (US TOUCH about CHF 35m to 40m of it), EMEA about CHF 91m (of which about CHF 30m is the full-year consolidation of KeriMedical and NeoOrtho), APAC and LATAM about CHF 23m. Revenue growth is therefore fairly diffuse by region. The change in profit is far more concentrated: US TOUCH carries the highest incremental gross margin, and three below-EBIT normalisations (tax, financing share, minorities) contribute more than a quarter of EPS growth. For the Consumer's D-03 question, the forecast differences rest on four drivers: US TOUCH volume and price, the selling-cost ratio (management's reinvestment choice), the tax rate, and the depreciation ratio. The first two are operational and observable each half-year; the last two are accounting outcomes that arrive only with the annual report.

The skew of outcomes over 18 months (to the H1 2027 results).

  • Revenue: upward (company habit of beat-and-raise; TOUCH ahead of plan) by 1 to 3 points of growth; the downside is a US administrator or channel pause (Bonesupport precedent) or an EMEA comparison effect in H1 2027.
  • EBITDA margin: centred on my base, with a downside tail from P-01's asymmetry.
  • EPS: wide (±25% to 30%) and, against consensus, downward, because consensus requires revenue, margin, depreciation and tax all at the favourable end.

Forecasts and upgrades

8

Probably upgrades this financial year to

–not yet rated

Memo topic 7, : the Rater's score

Behind the rating as a whole

Group judgement

The fourth set as a whole

D406th of 9

Layer 4 · The fourth set as a whole

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

The gap between our assessment and what is already reflected is favourable in the smallest of the three places and unfavourable in the two that carry more weight. Guidance is conservative against our forecasts by one to two points of growth, should be beaten for FY2026 and probably raised again in August 2027, and holds one real option, a change in the margin wording on 2 March; but it stops at EBITDA, and its pattern is expected. Sell-side estimates are above us on every line and by a widening margin down the income statement: 1% on FY2026 revenue, 5% on FY2027 revenue, 20% and 35% on EPS. The FY2026 gap is a single line, the tax rate, and it will be settled on 2 March 2027; unless the H2 tax rate falls to the low teens, the first event in the window is a growth beat and an earnings miss, followed by cuts to FY2027 and FY2028 EPS. The narrative knows the good news in full and the awkward facts hardly at all, so the surprises still available are skewed towards the earnings lines.

Put against the memo's topics: there is scope for upgrades to guidance over the next 6 to 12 months, little scope for upgrades to the brokers' revenue, and a likelihood of downgrades to their EPS; current momentum in estimates and narrative is strong but rests on two brokers, one release and a tax assumption; and management's expectation management is conservative where it guides and encouraging where it does not.

Three things keep this from being a clearly negative reading. First, this market has already shown, in 2026, that it will look through a miss below EBIT while growth accelerates, and growth will be seen to accelerate on 2 March. Second, our own EPS cone is wide and its upper end (management right on tax, margin released) comes close to the brokers; the direction of the business is not in dispute, only the timing, by about a year and a half. Third, the durable part of the case, a protected product in a three-year payment window with no rival, is if anything better founded than the narrative assumes. My steer to the Consumer: the investment case is strongest as a case about revenue and franchise, and weakest as a case about upgrades; over the next six months the headline is likely to be good and the earnings revision bad; over months 6 to 18 the test moves to whether growth holds in the first year of hard comparisons while margin and tax begin to deliver. The share price today pays for both to go right.

8a

Guidance

I-01

How likely is company guidance to be raised, met or reduced at the next few earnings events (all such events over the next 18 months), and what would drive each outcome? Secondarily, how likely is any guidance, mid-term target or ambition for 18 months to three years out to be raised, met or reduced?

A621st of 9

Layer 4 · Guidance

Judgement. Guidance is the one place where the gap between our assessment and what is published is favourable: the company guides growth below what we expect and has a two-year habit of raising it. Over the three earnings events in the window (2 March 2027, about mid-August 2027, about March 2028) I expect FY2026 guidance to be beaten, the first FY2027 growth range to be set below our forecast and then, at roughly even odds, raised at the half-year, and I put the chance of any cut to growth guidance in the whole window at about 15%. But the favourable gap is narrow in three ways. Guidance covers only two lines, organic growth and the EBITDA margin; it says nothing about the lines where our forecast differs most from the brokers'. The margin wording, "high teens at constant currency", is a ceiling that our own FY2027 margin already touches. And the pattern is by now expected, while the two brokers' forecasts already sit above anything the company is likely to guide.

Event by event (my probabilities).

Event Outcome Probability What would drive it
2 March 2027: FY2026 delivered Organic growth above the 17% to 19% range 85% Already signalled on 2 November; H2 has KeriMedical in the base, the easy Florida comparison and about 2,100 US TOUCH procedures
Margin "high teens at constant currency" met, at the upper end 90% Tariff cost lower in H2; one AlphaSense report, citing the 2 November release, says the company expects overachieving sales to lift the margin to the upper end of guidance
2 March 2027: first FY2027 guide, growth 15% to 17% 50% The habit of a prudent first range (13% to 15% for FY2025) and a year of harder comparisons
16% to 18% 30% The FY2026 precedent, when the first range stepped straight up; a strong TOUCH exit rate
17% to 19% or higher 10%
14% to 16% or lower 10% Visible US elective weakness or a soft start to the year
2 March 2027: first FY2027 guide, margin wording "High teens at constant currency", unchanged 55% The habit; room kept for US education cost and reinvestment
"Around 20%" or an explicit improvement on 2026 35% The finance director's mandate; Straumann's practice of guiding a margin step; tariff cost gone
A firm figure of 20% or more 10%
About mid-August 2027: H1 2027 Growth range raised 50% US TOUCH at or above about 3,000 procedures in the half; US plating growth in the mid-teens; EMEA at 10% or better against the winter-trauma comparison
Held 40% TOUCH on plan but EMEA slowed by the comparison; this is my base case if the first range is 16% to 18%
Lowered 10% A US administrator or elective pause; a supply bottleneck at KeriMedical; a channel problem
Margin wording raised 15% to 20% Straumann raised its 2026 margin guide in June; the same finance tradition
About March 2028: FY2027 delivered Above the top of the first range 45%
Within it 45%
Below it 10%
About March 2028: first FY2028 guide A lower growth range than the first FY2027 range 60% TOUCH comparisons harden; our FY2028 organic forecast is about 15%

Three points behind the table.

  1. "Raised" is consistent with our base case, not upside to it. Our FY2027 organic growth of about 17% sits at the top of the most likely first range. A raise in August 2027 to 16% to 18% would bring guidance up to our forecast, not above it. Guidance is therefore conservative against our assessment by about one to two points of growth, no more.
  2. The margin wording is the tell on 2 March. "High teens at constant currency" literally allows at most about 19.8% at constant currency, about 19.5% reported: our FY2027 margin exactly, and below the two brokers' 20.1%. If the wording is unchanged (my 55% case), our margin sits at the ceiling of guidance and the brokers' sits above it. If it moves to "around 20%" (35%), management has signalled that it will let leverage through, which is the first evidence for the undemonstrated strength of D-02 and supports the brokers' margin, though not their EPS.
  3. The first lower range matters more than any single miss. On my numbers the first FY2028 guide, in March 2028, is more likely than not to be lower than the year before. It would be the first time under this chief executive that the opening range steps down. By the sector's pattern (Set 1, C-84) that is the moment the market stops paying for acceleration, and it falls inside the window.

What guidance will not tell us. There is no guidance on EPS, tax, the financial result or free cash flow, and none is likely. The company cannot therefore "miss guidance" on the lines where I expect disappointment against the brokers. This is why a year can be a guidance beat and an earnings miss at once, as FY2025 was (growth of 15.7% against a 14% to 16% range; core net profit 36% below the consensus of the time).

Secondarily: 18 months to three years out.

  • There are no mid-term financial targets. I put the chance that the company sets some by mid-2028, at a capital markets day or with results, at about 35%. If it does, the Straumann tradition suggests targets a cautious analyst can already meet: mid-teens organic growth, an EBITDA margin "above 20%" by 2029 or 2030, positive free cash flow. That would be at or below the two brokers (21.3% in FY2028, 22.4% in FY2029) and in line with the model.
  • The US ambition of organic growth above 20% a year is likely to be met in 2027 and 2028 (about 75%), carried by TOUCH, and is at risk from 2029, when the base is larger and the US TOUCH price resets after pass-through. Its quality is weaker than its headline: excluding TOUCH, US growth in H1 2026 was about 7% to 14%.
  • The 18% to 19% long-term tax rate is the only forward statement below EBITDA. I put the chance that the core rate is at or below 20% by FY2028 at under 25%.
  • Capital spending "improving" after 2026 is likely to be met as a share of sales, not in francs.

How favourable is the gap? Favourable but small and largely anticipated: guidance will probably prove conservative on growth by one to two points, the pattern of raise-and-beat should hold at least through August 2027, and a change in the margin wording is a real, under-appreciated option on 2 March. The narrowness is that guidance stops at EBITDA.

I-02

How likely are sell-side consensus forecasts, and the net breadth of revisions, to move higher or lower over the next few earnings events (over the next 18 months): to be upgraded, cut or held, and why? Secondarily, how likely are the consensus forecasts for 18 months to three years out to be upgraded, cut or held?

F289th of 9

Layer 4 · Sell-side estimates

Judgement. Over the next three earnings events the two brokers' forecasts are more likely to fall than rise on every line, and by an amount that grows down the income statement: revenue trimmed by a few per cent, EBITDA by somewhat more, EPS cut materially. The net breadth of EPS revisions, strongly positive today (FY2027 EPS up 15% in one month and 18% in six), is more likely than not to turn negative at the first event, 2 March 2027. The reason is specific and near: for FY2026 we agree with the brokers on every line down to pre-tax profit, and the whole 20% gap in EPS is one line, the tax rate. The brokers' FY2026 EPS of CHF 1.55 implies an H2 2026 tax rate of about 13%, after 46% in H1. Unless that happens, FY2026 will be a growth beat and an earnings miss, as FY2025 was, and the brokers will have to carry a higher tax rate into FY2027 and FY2028, where they are already above us on revenue and margin as well.

Where the two brokers stand against the forecasts (my arithmetic on the changed inputs; FactSet medians).

FY2026: ours / brokers FY2027: ours / brokers FY2028: ours / brokers
Revenue 338.5 / 342 (-1%) 394 / 416 (-5%) 454 / 489 (-7%)
EBITDA 63.0 / 64.8 (-3%) 76.9 / 83.7 (-8%) 93.1 / 103.9 (-10%)
EBIT (ours before about CHF 2m of acquired-intangible amortisation) 33.5 / 33.7 42.9 / 49.7 (-14%) 54.6 / 68.7 (-21%)
Pre-tax profit 26.5 / 25.9 (+2%) 31.3 / 39.1 (-20%) 42.5 / 59.3 (-28%)
Tax rate (brokers' implied, including minorities) 42% / about 29% 35% / about 21% 30% / about 20%
Net income 15.3 / 18.4 (-17%) 20.3 / 31.0 (-35%) 29.8 / 47.2 (-37%)
EPS 1.24 / 1.55 (-20%) 1.63 / 2.50 (-35%) 2.37 / 3.79 (-37%)

The brokers' median net income and median EPS for FY2026 imply slightly different share counts, which is why the two FY2026 gaps differ.

What the table says.

  1. FY2026 is a tax question. H1 2026 core pre-tax profit was CHF 12.4m and core tax CHF 5.7m. The brokers' full-year figures (pre-tax CHF 25.9m, net income CHF 18.4m) leave about CHF 1.8m of tax for H2 on about CHF 13.5m of pre-tax profit. I see three possibilities: the brokers know something about H2 tax that the half-year figures do not show (this is where the unverified "12%" remark in the AlphaSense reports would fit, and why it must be checked); their figures are on a basis that differs from the company's core (FactSet's FY2025 "actual" on their basis was CHF 0.184 against core of about CHF 0.49, so this is plausible and would itself produce a recorded miss); or they have simply applied a normal full-year rate without regard to H1. On the second and third, FY2026 EPS is recorded well below CHF 1.55 on 2 March.
  2. FY2027 is 60% operating and 40% tax. Of the CHF 0.87 gap in FY2027 EPS, about CHF 0.50 comes from pre-tax profit (revenue 5% lower, margin 0.6 points lower, financing cost CHF 1m higher) and about CHF 0.37 from the tax rate alone. Operating gearing does the rest: a 5% difference in revenue becomes 8% at EBITDA, 14% at EBIT and 20% at pre-tax profit, because the margin is a little lower and depreciation and financing cost are fixed.
  3. Depreciation is not the difference Set 2 said it was, until FY2028. The brokers' own EBITDA less their own EBIT gives depreciation of about CHF 31m and CHF 34m in FY2026 and FY2027, close to ours. The separate FactSet depreciation line that Set 2 quoted (CHF 27.6m, 30.8m) is inconsistent with the brokers' EBIT. Only in FY2028 (about CHF 35m against our CHF 40m including acquired intangibles) is depreciation a real gap.

Why the brokers are where they are.

  • They updated on 2 and 3 November, the days of the TOUCH release, and extrapolated: FY2027 revenue growth of 21.6% assumes organic growth near 20% again, in the first year without the mechanical help that lifts H2 2026.
  • Their tax rates follow management, who guide 18% to 19% "long term" and have told analysts that higher rates are too conservative (Set 2). This is worth stating plainly for the memo's topic on how the company manages expectations: management is conservative in what it guides (growth, margin) and encouraging in what it does not guide (tax). The brokers' revenue sits above guidance by their own choice; their tax sits below the reported rate at management's prompting.
  • Their upper-extremities and North America figures (CHF 307m and about CHF 98m in FY2027) imply more than 7,000 US TOUCH procedures or a higher price than Set 2's.

My probabilities for the brokers' numbers over the window.

Line Cut Held Raised
FY2026 EPS as recorded against CHF 1.55 (2 March 2027) 65% (more than 10% below) 25% (within 10%) 10%
FY2027 revenue (CHF 416m) 55% (by 2% or more) 30% 15%
FY2027 EBITDA (CHF 83.7m) 60% 28% 12%
FY2027 EPS (CHF 2.50) 70% (by more than 10%) 20% 10%
FY2028 EPS (CHF 3.79) 70% (by more than 15%) 20% 10%
Net breadth of EPS revisions negative in the three months after 2 March 2027 65%

The likely sequence.

  • 2 March 2027. FY2026 organic growth beats guidance; reported revenue is in line with or slightly below the brokers (UBS has CHF 347m); EBITDA is about 3% below; EPS is about 20% below unless H2 tax collapses. The FY2027 guide is below the brokers on growth. By habit the brokers will keep growth above a guide they regard as prudent, so revenue changes little; but if the margin wording is unchanged they trim margin, and if the tax charge is high they raise tax. A tax rate moved from about 21% to about 30% alone cuts their FY2027 EPS by about 12%. My central expectation for the revision to FY2027 EPS at this event is minus 10% to minus 20%.
  • About mid-August 2027. The first half with hard comparisons. On our numbers revenue grows 16.5% against the brokers' 21.6% for the year; revenue is cut by 3% to 5% and EPS again by more.
  • About March 2028. FY2028 forecasts are reset to a first guide that is probably lower than the year before.

This repeats the pattern of March 2026, when the brokers raised revenue and cut EPS by double digits after the profit miss; the difference now is that their revenue leaves no room either.

What would make me wrong (the 10% to 15% cases). The tax rate really does fall to the low teens in H2 2026 and stays near management's 18% to 19%: that alone closes the FY2026 gap and about 40% of the FY2027 gap. US TOUCH runs at 9,000 or more procedures in 2027, or its price is nearer the list price than Set 2 assumed. Or management changes the margin wording and lets leverage through. Any one is possible; the brokers need all three.

A caution on "consensus" itself. FactSet's figures rest on two brokers; five houses are named as covering (ZKB, UBS, Stifel, Research Partners and Octavian). The share of buy ratings fell from 100% to 75% within the last three months, so one of four rated houses moved off Buy after the share price rose; no source identifies which, or why. With so few contributors a single broker's change, or the addition of a third contributor, will move "consensus" and the breadth measure sharply in either direction, whatever the fundamentals.

A knock-on for the valuation screen. If the brokers' two-year-forward EPS fell by 30% at an unchanged share price, the forward P/E would rise from about 30 times to about 43 times, and the stock would no longer screen as cheap against its own history (D-07).

Secondarily: 18 months to three years out. The brokers' FY2028 EPS is 37% above ours and the single broker's FY2029 EPS of CHF 5.60 is 43% above (FY2029 revenue of CHF 607m, up 24% on FY2028, comes from one broker and is not comparable with the two-broker years). These years rest on a tax rate near 20%, a margin of 21% to 22% and, from 2029, no visible allowance for the US TOUCH price reset after pass-through ends on 31 December 2028. I put the chance that FY2028 and FY2029 EPS forecasts are lower in 18 months than today at about 70%. The direction of the eventual business is not in dispute (our FY2030 EPS is about CHF 4.10); the brokers have it arriving about a year and a half earlier than we do.

11d

Current multiple

–not yet ratedMemo topic 11, : the Rater's score1 answer
D-0758
D-07

To what extent do the share price and the multiple move with sector and industry peers rather than idiosyncratically (on a rolling six-month basis), and so how far is the multiple company-driven rather than industry-driven?

A581st of 9

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement. The share price is overwhelmingly company-driven. Over the latest six-month window (26 May to 17 November 2026) the basket of dashboard peers explains 6% of the daily variance of Medartis's share price; 94% is the stock's own. Its correlation with the basket is 0.24 and its beta 0.49; it rose about 40% to 45% (the dashboard gives +38.7% over six months to 13 November, the co-movement table +45.1% to 17 November) while the basket rose 12.5%. Over 24 months of rolling six-month windows the share explained by peers has never exceeded 19% and has usually been 5% to 10%. The multiple is company-driven in the way that matters: its level, twice the sector's, is a premium paid for Medartis's own growth, and it has no support from the sector beneath it. One qualification: the multiple looks low against the stock's own history only on the two brokers' EPS, so it is as much "broker-driven" as company-driven.

The evidence.

  • Co-movement with individual peers is low everywhere, including where one would expect it. With Medacta, the closest business, the correlation is 0.17; with Bonesupport, the closest situation, 0.13; with Straumann 0.14. The highest is 0.32 (Convatec).
  • It is not a constant. The share explained by peers was 14% to 19% in the windows ending April to August 2026 and has fallen back to 6% as the stock moved on its own news (the H1 results of 18 August and the TOUCH statement of 2 November). Sector influence can therefore triple; it should be expected to rise in a broad sell-off.
  • Part of the low reading is illiquidity. The stock trades about 14,000 shares a day (about CHF 1.5m). Thin trading lowers measured daily correlation and enlarges the jumps on news.
  • The multiple's level. On the dashboard, Medartis's P/E is 36.9 times against a sector median of 17.4 times; only Bonesupport (33.5 times) is close. Fifteen of the 21 peers sit below the 20th percentile of their own ten-year P/E history: the sector as a whole has been de-rated. Medartis's own percentile is 13 (ten years) and 4 (five years, on FactSet's series, 30.3 times against a median of 45.6 times).
  • How the price rose. The 45% rise in twelve months came with rising estimates, not a rising multiple: the two brokers' FY2027 EPS is up 18% in six months and the forward window has rolled into FY2028. The multiple on the brokers' numbers is flat to lower.

What follows.

  1. The multiple will be set by Medartis's own results, on three dates. Sector rotation explains little. This is favourable for an investor with a view on the company and it means the case will not be rescued or sunk by the sector.
  2. There is no sector floor under the premium. A beta of 0.49 does not mean low risk; it means the risk is the company's own. If the company-specific story weakens, the reference multiple is the sector's 17 times, which is where Medacta (17.4 times, at the bottom of its ten-year range, shares down 27% in a year) now sits after its growth halved.
  3. "Low against its own history" depends on whose EPS. At CHF 109 the shares are 30 times the brokers' two-year-forward EPS, and 48 times the model's (about 52 times on my changed inputs), which is between the median (46.6 times) and the upper quartile (53.5 times) of the ten-year history. The dashboard's valuation rating of A is therefore a statement about the brokers' estimates.
  4. Earnings risk and multiple risk are the same risk. Because the multiple is company-driven, the events that would lower EPS (growth slowing in the first year of hard comparisons, no margin release, tax not falling) are the events that would lower the multiple. The model's return to end-2027 (+72% at update 1) applies the guideline multiple of 55.4 times to FY2029 EPS. I do not comment on the guideline, but the Consumer should know the sensitivity: on my changed inputs (FY2029 EPS about CHF 3.20) the same multiple gives about CHF 177 (+63%); the ten-year median multiple gives about CHF 149 (+37%); the ten-year 10th percentile (35.9 times) gives about CHF 115 (+5%). Ten points of multiple are worth about 29 points of return, more than all my input changes together.
11h

Estimated TSR [to nearest 5% band]

–not yet ratedMemo topic 12, : the Rater's score

Outside the eleven ratings

Answers not placed under any of the eleven ratings

The table the ratings map uses places these nowhere; each is shown with the reason the table records.

Why it is not placed It asks whether the stock fits the sector's historical patterns of share price and financial outperformance, a cross-check across many topics rather than one of the eleven.

C-84

What do historical precedents, rules of thumb and the empirical evidence show about how companies in this sector and industry have typically generated share price, financial and operational outperformance, and underperformance, and to what extent does this company fit those patterns?

C+5610th of 36

Layer 1 · Industry and Value Chain

Judgement. The patterns in specialist orthopaedics and Swiss medtech are clear, and Medartis currently fits the outperformance pattern closely, which is also why it carries the matching underperformance risk. Outperformance comes from accelerating organic growth through share gains, confirmed by beat-and-raise guidance and upward estimate revisions; underperformance comes from the first visible deceleration or the first guidance cut, and is severe because multiples are high and earnings thin. Viewforth's own Wisdom Library already names Medartis as an archetype of "growth too fast to forecast" (hypergrowth punctuated by stumbles and multiple compression).

The precedents and rules.

  1. Beat-and-raise is the share-price trigger in this sector (Sector digest: "a results beat alone does not lift a share price; watch for the first beat-and-raise the share price rewards"). Medartis has delivered beat-and-raise twice and a pre-announced beat; the share price rose 45% in twelve months and 16.6% in three months, with the jump after the 2 November TOUCH statement (price from about CHF 91 on the 50-day average to CHF 109). Fit: yes.
  2. A payment decision creates the market; revenue lags about a year. TOUCH fits exactly (code and pass-through January 2026; Australia July 2026). Fit: yes; implies strongest TOUCH revenue effect in 2027.
  3. Deceleration is punished hard. Medacta grew 18.5% at constant currency in FY2025, slowed to 9.7% in H1 2026, and fell 27% to a P/E at the bottom of its ten-year range. Medartis itself had a profit warning in 2019 (growth 8%, EBITDA margin 15% to 13%), exited China in 2022, and cut FY2024 guidance; each was followed by derating. Fit: high risk once growth slows.
  4. Unhedged Swiss exporters: reported earnings follow the franc; brokers and prices follow reported earnings (Geography digest; Straumann's reported growth 400 to 500 basis points below organic, targets CHF 81 to CHF 120). Fit: yes, more acutely, because Medartis has thinner margins and zero hedges.
  5. Capital-intensive consignment models get tolerated while growth is high. Medacta runs about CHF 1 of capex per CHF 1 of sales growth, FCF/EBITDA of 24% to 31%, and the market paid for it until growth slowed. Medartis has the same structure (inventory 36% of sales, sets capitalised and depreciated over five years). Fit: yes.
  6. Specialist medtech margins expand with scale and gross-margin mix, rarely from cost programmes alone (Straumann's margin came with share and pricing; Smith & Nephew's cost programmes fight scale disadvantage). Medartis's margin path depends on scale leverage of selling costs, which has not shown yet.
  7. Founder-controlled Swiss medtech: governance discount, but stable strategy (Medacta 69% family; Medartis 39.9% founder with opting-out). Fit: yes.

Conclusion. Medartis is in the sweet spot of pattern 1 and 2 right now and is exposed to patterns 3 and 4. The Consumer should model a base case that holds growth well above market but should carry explicitly the precedent that a halving of growth from these levels has cost peers 25% to 40% of their share price.

Why it is not placed The table holds this question in reserve for Riskiness sub-items that are not yet built, so it is not yet placed.

C-83

How far could the shares fall to a fair trough multiple?

D3424th of 36

Layer 1 · Risks

Judgement. In a "growth stumble" (organic growth slowing to low teens or below for two halves, margins flat), a fair trough is about CHF 65 to 80, a fall of roughly 25% to 40% from CHF 109. In a "false friend confirmed" case (single-digit growth, margins compressing, cash negative), CHF 45 to 55, a fall of 50% to 60%, which is low probability. A pure de-rating with fundamentals intact (sector-wide sell-off) would take it to about CHF 80 to 90.

Basis.

  • Price anchors: CHF 56.60 at end-2024 (before the re-rating); 52-week low CHF 70.60; CHF 89.40 in July 2026.
  • Multiple anchors: the closest peer, Medacta, fell 27% to a P/E at the bottom of its ten-year range (17.4x) when growth halved; the Risks report's bear case re-rates EV/EBITDA from about 18x to 11x for a 35% fall. Medartis's own 24-month-forward P/E is 30.3x, at the 4th percentile of its five-year range (range 27.7x to 166x): "cheap against its own history" is an artefact of historically depressed EPS, not a floor.
  • Enterprise-value arithmetic: at 3.0x to 3.5x EV/forward sales (about where the stock traded at its 52-week low) on FY2027 sales of CHF 375m to 416m, less about CHF 117m of net debt (FactSet's EV bridge), over 14.7m diluted shares, the equity is worth about CHF 69 to 91. A 2.5x multiple on CHF 350m gives about CHF 52.
  • P/E cross-check: stumble-case FY2027 EPS of about CHF 1.8 to 2.1 at 30x to 35x gives CHF 55 to 74.

Why it is not placed It rates the staircase of news the market can look forward to, and none of the eleven rates the narrative or the catalysts as such.

Group judgement

Stock market narrative

A642nd of 36

Layer 1 · Stock market narrative

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

The narrative ahead is rich in dated, novel and mostly well-founded events over 18 months: a likely beat and the first FY2027 guide in March 2027, TOUCH compounding as reimbursement takes hold, tariffs disappearing from the cost base, and the investment cycle peaking. That is the classic staircase on which specialist medtech stocks re-rate. The narrative risk is that much of the near-term staircase (FY2026 beat, TOUCH upgrades) is already in the price and in two brokers' numbers after a 45% rise, so the steps that can still surprise are margin release and cash, which are exactly the ones management has been least willing to promise.

Why it is not placed It rates how well the earlier answers agree with each other, which is a check on the analysis rather than on the company.

D-01

How congruent are the answers to all the questions in the earlier sets: to what extent do they support and align with each other, rather than contradict or jar?

–56

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement. The earlier answers are congruent in their architecture and incongruent in several of their facts. On the shape of the case they agree, and the agreement runs across independent lines of evidence: a strong, visible top line (demand, the TOUCH franchise, the raise-and-beat record and the revenue model all say the same thing), weak economics below gross profit (returns, cash, earnings quality and the EPS record all say the same thing), and a market that has already rewarded the first and not yet tested the second. That coherence is worth something: the case has one fault line, not many, and every set finds it in the same place, between revenue and EPS. But six facts that the forecasts lean on were wrong or unsettled in the earlier sets, and almost every correction moves the same way, towards lower EPS and less balance-sheet room. The pattern of the errors is itself the finding: the earlier sets were most reliable where the company discloses most (revenue, units, gross margin) and least reliable where it discloses least (tax, debt, price), and that is exactly where the forecast's EPS is decided.

Where the sets support each other (the load-bearing agreements).

  • Revenue. Set 1's demand, cycle and guidance answers (C-01, C-06, C-13, C-80) and Set 2's revenue model (P-10) agree on about 19.5% to 20% organic growth in FY2026, about 17% in FY2027 and a fade to low teens, led by the US and TOUCH.
  • Earnings quality. Set 1's economic-strength group (C-49 to C-54) and Set 2's below-EBIT work (P-06, P-08) agree that EPS is the least predictable line and that the largest misses have come from tax, currency and financing.
  • Guidance. Both sets read the guidance architecture the same way: the growth range is a floor, the "high teens" margin wording is a ceiling.

Where they jar, and how I resolve each.

Issue Set 1 Set 2 My resolution
Operating leverage Not demonstrated; model below the brokers "Close to achievable"; same incremental margin as the brokers (25% to 29%) against a six-year average of 21.5% Both are right about different things. The rate is defensible for FY2027, where most of the 0.9-point gain has a mechanical source (tariff, TOUCH mix). Four consecutive years at 0.9 points is above what the comparable Swiss education-led specialist achieved while growing (D-02). I keep the input and treat the risk as skewed down
Direction of consensus revenue Brokers' FY2027 revenue "full" Says both "lean up against guidance, not against consensus" (P-06) and "revenue upgrades are likely" (P-10) while its own FY2027 revenue is 5.2% below the brokers The second statement does not follow from Set 2's own numbers. Guidance raises are likely; upgrades to the brokers' revenue are not (I-02)
Tax 22% to 26% as a normal rate 46% observed, falling to 28% in FY2027 on the view that the leakage is a fixed sum H1 2026 disproves the fixed-sum view: tax in one half (CHF 5.7m) exceeded all of FY2025 (CHF 5.2m) at the same 46% rate. Input changed
Debt Bank CHF 12m; net financial debt CHF 84m Bank about CHF 32m; CHF 104m Bank CHF 58.1m; CHF 132m; plus CHF 20.4m of earn-outs. Inputs changed
